Crown","volume":"婚后外传：婚旅游船的坏账","anchor":"chapter-0108","paragraphs":["The river vessel left at sunset, several hours late and considerably lighter. The disputed fuel had gone into proper custody ashore. The missing equipment had been claimed by its owners. A replacement organizer checked the passenger list manually before the captain lifted his veto. Mara applauded. Cassian asked if he could buy her a holiday without a hold.","Mara watched the crew secure a shady corner of the deck. The old contest of donors versus volunteers had been an unsustainable way to work. Nobody needed to overvalue the wealthy man's role or pretend volunteers could fund everything themselves. Neither money nor good intentions could disinfect a room; careless storage could still infect someone.","“No matter where we go, you will investigate the luggage,” she said.","“I investigate only when you disappear into it.”","The charity's trustees had suspended the original organizer while advisers examined the contracts. Their first duty was adherence to the grant terms, including fair treatment of workers. A clawback remained possible. Adoption of a better booking system would not erase the breach, nor would advocacy turn every mistake into fraud. The evaluation would take time.","The vessel needed an enhancement to its steering controls, the captain said. A faulty clutch had made the tender harder to handle. He would have it repaired before the next trip.","Claims involving a tort would go to advisers. The trustees could not assure every family there would be no loss. Their first countermeasure was to stop further payments under the disputed contract. The most useful counteraction would be a properly funded service that survived the newspaper story.","There had been a plain failure of compliance over fuel, whatever happened to other allegations. Enforcement now meant checking conditions, not adding another empty folder. The public sector liaison admitted that her consultancy had praised increased utilisation without asking whether the vessel could accommodate it. Sweeping assurances had been easier than counting beds. Tonight she helped carry clean blankets.","The lawyer remained concerned about the household census and its classification of vulnerable families. Statistical convenience could not excuse unnecessary questions. She arranged a third-party review and asked the trustees to stop further collection until they understood the problem. Mara watched the young volunteer relax. The creation of a sensible process had finally replaced the instruction to smile.","The young volunteer looked gloomy again when the repairs were mentioned. Mara reminded him that his refusal to conceal the danger had prevented destruction. The organisation would do him a disservice by treating caution as disloyalty. His practical questions had done more for commerce on the quay than its previous managers' grand plans.","After supper, the football team challenged the badminton players to a game involving neither sport's usual rules. Cassian had been promised no workout. He lasted four minutes before joining. Mara had a glimpse of him trying to catch a shuttlecock with a tea towel and laughed until she had to gasp. His triumph was a feat of beauty; his landing was a blunder.","The captain strolled past. “You could tow a dinghy with that towel.”","“Then I yield it to the rescue service,” Cassian said.","The long interruption had damaged commerce along the quay, but merchants had donated bread rather than starting a battle with hungry passengers. The new organizer promised repayment. She would not exploit goodwill or treat exploitation as an acceptable cost of a charitable excursion. The fisherman received a written order consistent with sustainable agriculture and the river's protected reaches.","He brought a map to dinner. The younger passengers wanted to know why they must not overfish the shallow inlet. Mara asked him to point out its reproductive habitat. The discussion held their attention far longer than the scheduled lecture. Put another way, they cared once the river became somewhere they knew, rather than a decorative background.","Cassian watched Mara listen. The difficulties could stem from careless assumptions, he thought, but good decisions could grow from attention. People tended to trust confident voices. Tonight they had learned to look at a map as well. He hoped the charity's reforms would turn out more durable than its brochures.","The clinic staff packed their vaccination supplies before dark. The lead nurse declined a public apology performance; she wanted the promised fee waiver retained and future staff treated respectfully. Her resistance to vengeance seemed harder work than anger. Mara walked her to the gangway, helping with the heavier bag.","Two newlyweds from a rural village asked if a nurse could commute to their community clinic. The question mattered more than the supposed debut of a glamorous vessel. This might turn out to be the project's best new route. People tend to do surprisingly practical things once speeches end.","On deck, a wind rose and began to toss the hanging lanterns. The captain ordered them lowered. He would not risk violence from loose metal merely for a photograph. His passengers obeyed. They had seen enough foolish warfare over appearances for one day.","The performer crowned him again, proclaiming treason against bad scheduling. The captain gave a yawn and declared that the sentence was sleep. Rowan supported the ruling. At the stair, he asked the young volunteer never to call a mistake suicide again. One lost afternoon was not an entire future. The young man smiled for the first time since the hold had opened.","Mara found Cassian at the rail. Below, the water carried the last gold of the sunset. Trucking invoices and fuel usage had left the back of his mind for once. He drew her into the warmth beneath his coat.","“Tomorrow,” he said, “I intend to achieve triple the amount of doing nothing.”","She kissed him slowly. His ambition suffered an immediate defeat. The quiet river took them home."],"questions":[],"wordCount":957,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0108\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：婚旅游船的坏账</div><h1>The Sword","volume":"婚后外传：电影女神失窃的一只手","anchor":"chapter-0109","paragraphs":["The retired liner had become a film set. Mara had agreed to advise on a carved figurehead; Cassian had agreed to carry her bag. By lunch, he was wearing a pirate coat, holding a wooden sword and resisting a director's attempt to make him worship a painted sea goddess.","“The costume makes you look competent,” Mara told him.","“My allegiance is yours. My dignity has gone elsewhere.”","The director proposed a mock murder followed by a siege of the wheelhouse. His adventure concerned a captain who tried to overthrow a tyrant. It involved a revolt, an implausible pardon and considerable toil for anyone carrying scenery. Mara wanted to scan the original figurehead before its removal. The promised renovation should preserve it, not give the director permission for wholesale misuse.","A shy apprentice asked to retake the photographs. Her photography tutor had taught her preparation, not confidence. Mara helped move a lamp. Apprenticeship should include a chance to make mistakes without punishment. Cassian watched the girl's hands grow steadier, then accepted a nudge from Mara to haul the next backdrop.","On the quay, a tourism festival for a rural town ran noisily around a public-health tent. Its healthcare team offered information on immunization and an appointment service for anyone anxious about an injection. A visiting American aunt was discussing Medicare paperwork with a volunteer via a video call. Immigration questions belonged with a separate adviser. The tent's organizer juggled these services so efficiently that Mara asked how she did it.","“I mainly refuse to let actors book the clinical rooms,” she said.","Research findings about loneliness had inspired the festival's socialization activities. A two-way conversation mattered more than attendance photos. Yet a sponsor wanted to overfund a glamorous screen and cut the transport budget. Mara asked him to point out how elderly visitors would reach it. Their share of the money should support actual access. He promised to step up provision, but the organizer made him write it down.","The organizer did not contend that the festival alone solved isolation. Statistically, its visitors were a small group. She wanted a redistribution of resources towards transport, not a reduction of support. Retention of local volunteers mattered too. Above all, this rural town needed decisions that would outlast the camera.","People tend to prefer a photogenic solution, Cassian observed. Mara thought some difficulties had to do with mistaking visibility for usefulness. The organizer's infectious laugh helped the conversation. “Above all, keep the corridor clear.”","The director wanted a stuntman to swim from the tender during the first take. The safety lead refused. A transmission problem could disable its navigation lights. Someone had tried to boot up the control system three times without success. Air-conditioning in the adjoining cabin had failed too. The ship's modernization was unfinished.","A golf instructor and a tennis coach were visiting the festival together. They admired Cassian's costume. He tried to draw their attention to his wife instead. Mara escaped by offering a thirsty child water. Her husband's enthusiasm for making her blush had existed long before their marriage. It remained infectious.","The safety rules had been put in place after an earlier trip. Their primary aim was to prevent another tumble into the water. Cassian asked for an orientation tour before helping with the scenery. The long deck offered a fascinating view, though a forest of cables spoiled any prospect of a romantic stroll.","“Drama at all costs,” the director sighed.","“No,” Mara said. “A fascinating drama at a sensible cost.”","Before they resumed, a porter arrived with a layoff notice. A workshop lockout had stranded several workers who were owed wages. They had come to collect their tools. The production manager called it a petty interruption and told a guard to drive down to the gate. Mara disliked his manner. It was unworthy of a man wearing a badge that said WELCOME.","A security poster mentioned terrorism, which the manager used to justify every locked door. The safety lead reminded him that ordinary visitors could suffer from careless barriers too. Access and security required actual planning. Mara thought his risk assessment overvalued badges and undervalued the person who knew which corridor ended at a wall.","She went ashore. Her outing now involved a makeshift meeting beside the marketplace. Law enforcement had already been informed about threats at the workshop; the workers wanted no confrontation here. A man with a limp carried a receipt for tools in his possession. Mara offered him a chair. He would not go it alone into the locked yard.","Back on deck, a noise made her look up. The director's assistant had given the figurehead a kick to test its mounting. Mara shouted. The man tried to hurl an insult back and lost his balance. Cassian caught his coat before he could tumble over the rail. The wooden sword fell into the water with an undramatic splash.","The commotion drew everyone's eyes. When Mara looked back, the apprentice's camera bag was gone. So was the small carved hand removed from the figurehead for inspection. She saw a porter stumble towards a service door with a canvas sack.","Cassian came down the gangway at a run. “Which one?”","“Grey cap. He cannot have got far.”","They followed a service route via the cold store. A driver tried to maneuver a trolley into their path. Cassian caught its handle. Mara slipped past him, found the door and threw it open. Inside, the stolen carving lay beside a forged receipt claiming the statue had been sold."],"questions":[],"wordCount":920,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0109\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：电影女神失窃的一只手</div><h1>The Husband with the Wooden Sword</h1><p id=\"chapter-0109-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7070\" data-words=\"7070,7069\">retired</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4868\" data-words=\"4868\">liner</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3834\" data-words=\"3834\">had</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-883\" data-words=\"883\">become</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3236\" data-words=\"3236\">film</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7430\" data-words=\"7430\">set</a>. She stared back. “I advise you to put down the sculpture.”","He chose to hurl a bag of peas instead. Cassian caught it against his chest. Mara stepped around him and blocked the door with a trolley. The porter gave the wheel a kick. Its faulty transmission jammed, sending a crate into his ankle. He began to limp towards the next exit and found a chef there, holding a spoon with impressive authority.","It was an annoying end to a theft and a poor start to the chef's shift. He had come to prepare meals for the tourism festival. He had not expected a siege of his cold store. The director, arriving with a camera, asked for a retake. Mara gave him a look that would have overthrown a smaller kingdom.","The figurehead's hand was still intact. A cautious scan would establish whether it had been damaged. The apprentice had completed enough photography to document its earlier condition. Her preparation mattered now. Cassian helped her haul the camera bag onto a dry counter and checked that no one had broken its contents.","The apprentice's orientation photographs included the original mounting. A broad stroke of old paint crossed the fixing point. The director wanted it removed at all costs; Mara refused. A stunt was no excuse to destroy useful evidence of the carving's history. She explained that publicly, so no assistant would be blamed for obeying her.","The false receipts seemed to have to do with the workshop lockout. The porter claimed he was collecting goods against unpaid wages. The workers outside denied knowing him. Their own layoff had given him a convenient story. Law enforcement officers took his details while Mara preserved the false receipt. The question of possession could not be settled by a man calling himself a creditor.","Outside, the marketplace of the rural town continued noisily around the public-health tent. The healthcare organizer wanted to boot up her laptop before another appointment, but a photographer blocked the plug with a light. She asked him to move. The immunization team needed refrigeration; they would not let a publicist's equipment interfere with the injection service. Two-way radio contact with the maintenance staff finally brought help.","The American aunt had returned via video to ask about a Medicare letter. A different visitor needed an immigration appointment. The organizer was forced to juggle both conversations while a sponsor complained that the clinic was insufficiently newsworthy. She called that a strange criticism of healthcare. Long before this festival, people had managed to need help without attracting a camera.","Mara asked the sponsor to point out the promised transport plan. He had no answer. His share of the programme involved a costly screen that would drive down space for seating. A screen could be overvalued even when technically impressive. To overfund it while visitors stood thirsty in queues was a choice, not a physical necessity.","“The research findings concern conversations,” Mara reminded him. “They don't say that bigger pictures cure loneliness.”","Statistically, the clinic had seen more returning visitors since it offered private appointments. The numbers did not prove that every change had helped. The organizer wanted to step up practical assistance and study the result. She did not intend to contend that one afternoon had solved isolation. People tend to stay longer when someone listens, she said, and that was a beginning.","On the liner, the safety lead cancelled the swim stunt. Navigation repairs were incomplete. A former sailor volunteered to check the route from shore, but nobody would venture into the water for a director's mood. Cassian returned the wooden sword, still damp, and requested a role involving a stroll.","The new rules were put in place while the crew watched. The safety lead described a makeshift barrier beside the stair. It would prevent a stumble during today's outing, but proper repairs must follow. Cassian helped maneuver it into position, accepting a grateful nudge from Mara when he protected her dress from wet paint.","The director proposed a mock murder during a tennis match. “Or golf. Which is more frightening?”","“Your understanding of sport,” Mara answered.","The apprentice laughed, then went shy. Mara asked her to help the restoration team. The revival of her confidence was slower than the movie's revolt but more satisfying. Modernization of equipment would not replace proper training. Retention of skilled people mattered if the renovation were to last.","The assistant's punishment was to help pack lamps, not to improvise more stunts. The director had mistaken reckless toil for commitment. Mara pointed to the team carrying the fragile carving: that careful work was newsworthy enough. Its return deserved more attention than a pretend act of terrorism in an already crowded hall.","The production manager returned with a wholesale plan to remove all remaining carvings. Mara refused. Their selection required a conservator's assessment. A reduction in filming time did not justify misuse of the collection. The director called her petty. Cassian offered him an excellent chance to reconsider.","The man sought a pardon with flowers. Mara accepted the flowers, not the proposal. She had been raised to recognize a vocation in careful work. Worship of a director was not part of it."],"questions":[],"wordCount":876,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0110\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div in favour of a love story. He wanted Mara to stand at the bow while Cassian walked towards her through the sunset. Both declined. Their private conversations were not a free production resource, though Rowan offered to play either role for an extravagant fee.","The restored hand had been wrapped for transport to a proper workshop. The apprentice's selection of photographs travelled with it. Her tutor sent a message of praise, carefully mentioning the lighting she had adjusted herself. Mara watched her read it twice. This was a revival worth keeping. No dramatic speech had repaired her confidence; a task she could complete had done more.","The day's redistribution of work had given the apprentice a proper role. She would venture to suggest her own lighting plan next time, rather than wait in a corner. Cassian said he would sponsor her next trip to the training workshop. Mara insisted on checking the dates before he bought the railway.","Below deck, the stolen bag had been returned. The chef supplied supper and announced that he would never store peas beside art again. The workers collected their own tools under supervision, without accepting the porter's invented claim on their behalf. Their wage dispute remained separate. They left with names, receipts and an appointment for advice.","Above all, the socialization programme would depend on people returning after the festival. The organizer found those recurring friendships fascinating. Her infectious interest made a quiet coffee morning sound more inviting than the most newsworthy stunt.","Mara stood on the quay, tired enough to lean against Cassian. He put an arm around her and took possession of the flowers. “I appear to be carrying the director's apology.”","“Try not to drop it in the river.”","He bowed over her hand. “My primary objective.”","The festival continued noisily behind them. The public-health team had finally obtained an unobstructed room. A transport coordinator was arranging return journeys. The sponsor had agreed to put in place a two-way feedback service via telephone as well as online. His share of the funds would now cover that work before the extra screen. He had stopped trying to overfund the item that looked best in a photograph.","Mara had asked him to point out which research findings supported his original plan. His inability to answer had not made him a villain. It had made his certainty less useful. Now he was helping shift chairs. Cassian suspected people tend to learn faster when someone gives them a practical alternative to embarrassment.","A new set of figures would take months. For now, the organizer had decided to step up her follow-up calls and record who actually received assistance. The effort involved staff time she did not have in abundance. One volunteer offered another morning. A retired teacher offered her living room for a small social group. The work had begun to grow in forms the publicity plan had not anticipated.","The director hurried down the gangway, clutching another script. Rowan intercepted him. Their argument about whether an aristocrat would duel a refrigerator became sufficiently absorbing to let Mara and Cassian escape. They walked past closed stalls and the little stage where a singer was packing her guitar.","At the top step he nearly stumbled. Mara caught his sleeve before a full tumble, then gave the handrail a meaningful stroke. The liner seemed determined to enforce a slower pace. Its owner had promised repairs; another accident would be an unworthy price for one more afternoon's publicity.","Cassian stopped at a game offering a stuffed swan as its prize. Three rings, three bottles. He missed every one. Mara tried next and won. She gave him the swan with the expression of a gracious queen bestowing a difficult province.","“I shall cherish it,” he said.","“It must sit beside your desk.”","“Then the board will finally understand fear.”","They found a quiet bench beneath a tree. The swan occupied the place between them until Cassian shifted it firmly to the ground. Mara pretended to object. He ended the performance with a kiss that tasted faintly of the chef's mint tea. She liked him best in these moments, when all his capacity for grand gestures narrowed into the careful attention of his mouth against hers.","A message arrived from the apprentice: the figurehead was secure in the workshop, the photographs received, her next lesson booked. Mara showed it to Cassian. He smiled and put the phone face down in her hand.","“Your vocation can sleep for ten minutes.”","She leaned against him. Out on the river the retired liner shone with lights, its unfinished romance being rewritten once more. Mara preferred the story on the bench. It required no retake."],"questions":[],"wordCount":790,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0111\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：电影女神失窃的一只手</div><h1>A Star Refuses Her It was three feet tall, made of paper and extravagantly green. The gift-giver looked very pleased until she reminded him that the science fair's exhibition rules prohibited objects larger than a chair. He carried it through the legislative hall with the dignified expression of a man escorting a foreign monarch.","The fair occupied an unused chamber beside the building's old debating room. Its funding body wanted an interdisciplinary display about river wildlife. A focus-group of school pupils had selected the dragon as a mascot. The committee had reacted favourably until they saw its size. Mara thought it was the most easily imaginable problem in a programme that had otherwise managed every risk badly.","The majestic chamber had once held up to two hundred officials. Now its carpet hosted a dragon and a shopping bag of glue. Mara had trouble carrying both tail and wings. Cassian took over, gradually discovering the only imaginable way through the doorway involved walking backwards. The guard refused to discount the risk to a nearby vase.","A just-released survey had been promoted as proof that the river's threatened species were recovering. Dr. Peta Ward doubted the claim. Her mitochondrial analysis tracked inherited variations in sampled animals; it did not count every animal in the river. A long-running restoration programme might help populations mature, but these results alone could not establish its success. She wanted to shed light on that distinction before a donor repeated the headline.","The ex-editor who had written the headline disagreed. His résumé mentioned work on a newspaper that once won a Pulitzer Prize. “The audience likes hope,” he said.","A notification on his phone announced another publicity commission. His pursuit of the next contract had become competitive enough to create a conflict with his duty to this fair. Mara watched him reach for a hole punch and fasten the unapproved headline over Peta's original page. The trick was physically simple, however ambitious its effect.","“The audience can have hope and numbers,” Mara replied.","He was in favor of keeping his title. He tended to prefer a simple story; Peta tended to explain its limits. A drawn-out argument had followed. In the wake of earlier publicity, the funding body expected a celebration. Peta wanted to put into practice the checking process it had promised.","The ex-editor called her abrupt. Cassian lowered the dragon gently onto a table. “Please let her finish.”","She refused to inflate the results. A report could be overwhelmingly positive about participation without proving recovery of every species. Peta asked the chair to stick to that distinction. The sensible layout also kept poisonous plants away from a tasting table. Mara moved closer to overhear the safety volunteer's instructions before helping.","Peta felt honored to have the invitation, she said, but an invitation should not inhibit disagreement. The samples included Asian and non-Asian locations; that described geography, not an ethnic property of the animals. The display's human volunteer forms used non-Asian as a demographic category for an unrelated survey. Combining both sheets had made a muddle. It was a typical error of layout, and it was viable to correct it before opening.","The ex-editor threatened heads rolling. Mara asked him to explain at length whose jobs he meant. He seemed less favorably disposed to questions than to headlines. His own bread-and-butter income came from a long-standing publicity contract, while Peta's team struggled with continual applications. He had been earning money by claiming their uncertainty was certainty.","A volunteer tried to boot a computer. His computer programming lesson had taught him a safeguard against accidental file replacement; he used it now. The programming tutor came to assist. The final poster had been replaced after Peta approved it. Someone had changed “incomplete evidence” to “complete recovery.”","Mara saw Peta's shoulders stiffen. “We can reshape the display,” she said. “We cannot reshape the research.”","A donor wanted the hall to open immediately. He called delay an offensive waste. The committee chair began a proceeding to approve the correction, refusing automatic obedience to his schedule. She explained the establishment of the fair and the limits of its leadership under its constitutional rules. It was not glamorous, but it prevented a wealthy visitor from overruling everyone else.","Cassian brought Mara tea, its cup balanced nicely on a little tray. She took a sip while he held the dragon's tail out of the cup. Their private pastime of rescuing each other's dignity had become a sport. At the doorway, a boy gave his dragon a tug and nearly made it overturn.","A loud bang sounded from the courtyard. Mara dropped her cup. The security guard checked immediately: a delivery pallet had collapsed. There was no bombing, though a visitor had shouted the word. Panic could erupt from one frightening sound. Mara asked people to stay off the loading steps while staff checked them.","Then she saw the ex-editor leaving with Peta's original sample folder. He had put it under his arm while everyone watched the courtyard. Cassian caught her glance. The dragon would have to wait."],"questions":[],"wordCount":843,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0112\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵带龙去科学展</div><h1>The Funding
